Loyal and disciplined, they gladly carry the banner of Gondor across the face of Middle-earth.

{Gondor_Guardsmen}Guardias territoriales
{Gondor_Guardsmen_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nGondorian guardsmen are levied territorial troops whose main purpose is to defend the far flung outposts of their homeland, they operate under the control of local lords and assist in defending their home regions until the more advanced troops can be mustered. Though usually found in the less advanced territories of Gondor, they are prepared to defend any city during the absence of better trained and equipped troops. Technically, they can also be used to bolster the numbers of any active army. However, generals should note that these men are citizens first, and soldiers second; they will be overwhelmed by most regular army troops. 
{Gondor_Guardsmen_descr_short}Lightly armoured spearmen from all corners of Gondor.

{Gondor_Militia}Milicia de Gondor
{Gondor_Militia_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nConsisting of volunteers who wish to do their part in Gondor's defence, these militiamen are pretty well motivated and equipped, which somewhat compensates for their rudimentary training. They are prime candidates for police duties and deterring small groups of raiders. A good commander may be able to use them in more serious engagements as they can prove to be quite resilient with their solid shields and swords. 
{Gondor_Militia_descr_short}Strong militia units with solid shields and swords.

{Gondor_Infantry}Infantería de Gondor
{Gondor_Infantry_descr}Morale: Very Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nThe Realm of Gondor has fought numerous wars against numerous enemies. Their Lords are very experienced in the arts of war and fighting, and the citizens of Gondor are all expected to earn their keep when the horn of Gondor sounds. Most of the brave men of Gondor are trained as regular infantry, armed with a solid metal shield and a steel longsword. For centuries, the kingdom of Gondor has relied on the strong traditions of these heavy infantry. When the storm clouds begin to gather once again at the end of the Third Age, most of the Gondorian Infantry eagerly anticipate the opportunity of serving their country with arms in their hands. Armoured with full plate mail, they are the backbone of the Gondorian Army. 
{Gondor_Infantry_descr_short}Professional heavy infantry, armed with swords and shields, with excellent morale and stamina.

{Gondor_Spearmen}Lanceros de Gondor
{Gondor_Spearmen_descr}Morale: Very Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nUsually wielding 8 foot spears, these troops support Gondor's main infantry and are often placed on the flanks of a formation to protect against cavalry assaults. Similarly equipped as their sword-wielding kinsmen, they wear plate armour, with the symbolic White Tree embossed on the breast-plate. Provided with a heavy oak shield reinforced with steel, they are well defended from arrow fire and close combat. These competent troops won't think twice before laying down their lives in the protection of The White City. 
{Gondor_Spearmen_descr_short}Professional heavy spearmen protected with shields and plate armour and armed with a solid spear.

{Gondor_Pikemen}Piqueros de Gondor
{Gondor_Pikemen_descr}Morale: Good \nMorale Response: Good \nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain Bonus: Grassland \nTerrain Malus: Barren, Snow \nBonus Against: Camels, Wargs, Horses, Oliphaunts\n\nFor a long time Gondor never had to deal with enemies using overwhelming numbers of cavalry, since the greatest horse lords of Middle-earth, the Rohirrim, are trusted allies of the Kingdom of Gondor. But the constant skirmishes with the men of Harad and Khand have forced the Gondorians to adapt to better counter enemy cavalry. Based on the famous Pikemen of Dol Amroth, Gondor Pikemen use long pikes and depend on their heavy plate armour for defense. Thanks to their training and discipline, these pikemen can easily stop any enemy foolish enough to assault them from the front, but their tight formation makes them susceptible to ranged attacks and flanking maneuvers.
{Gondor_Pikemen_descr_short}Professional heavy pikemen protected with plate armour and armed with a solid pike.

{Gondor_Archers}Arqueros de Gondor
{Gondor_Archers_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nAccuracy: Average\nRange: 170 meters\nMissiles: 24\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nAlthough these archers are not as skilled as Elves, nor do they wield the fabled hollow steel bow of the Númenóreans, they should still be a force to reckon with. The strength and stature of these archers means they can shoot a Longbow with ease, even if they’re clad in full plate armour. The armour means they need a longer time to reload their great bows and are slow to move, but they will stand their ground in melee combat. Armed with a sword and trained as Gondorian Infantry, they fear few enemies and are expected to fend off most attacking infantry. 
{Gondor_Archers_descr_short}Professional heavy archers, armed with a longbow and a sword for close combat. Wearing full plate armour.

{Gondor_Cavalry}Caballería de Gondor
{Gondor_Cavalry_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Forest, Snow\n\nHorsemanship has never been the main interest of the Gondorian army. Often they’ve received mounted aid from their allies, the Rohirrim in the west, who are unmatched when it comes to the art of fighting from horseback. The Warlords and Generals of Gondor do however understand that cavalry is an important part of any army, and will usually deploy a unit of Gondor Cavalry when the need arises. They wear the standard full plate armour of the Gondorian army and wield a longsword and shield making them well suited for close combat. 
{Gondor_Cavalry_descr_short}Professional medium cavalry armed with swords and shields and protected by plate armour.

{Pinnath_Cavalry}Caballería de Pinnath Gelin
{Pinnath_Cavalry_descr}Morale: Very Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nSecondary Attack: 6\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Forest, Snow\n\nThe fiefdom of Pinnath Gelin, the Green Hills, is a peaceful and pleasant region of Gondor, sheltered from both the threat of Mordor to the east and the frequent raids of the Corsairs of Umbar to the south. But this peace will soon come to it’s end, for Sauron is once again gathering his forces and prepares to cover all in his shadow. \n\n The soldiers of Pinnath Gelin are known to be courageous men, skilled with the use of the spear and the sword, and determined to protect their beautiful home, and Gondor, with all they have. The cavalry of Pinnath Gelin in particular are some of the finest mounted warriors of Gondor, outside of the Swan Knights of the Prince of Dol Amroth. Armoured in fine chainmail, and proudly wearing the green tabbards of their home, they will ride out against Gondor’s enemies and fight with skill and fervour. \n\nREQUIRES SERELOND OR CIRITH IAUR. 
{Pinnath_Cavalry_descr_short}Professional cavalry from the green hills of Pinnath Gelin armed with lances, swords and protected by sturdy chainmail. \n\nREQUIRES SERELOND OR CIRITH IAUR.

{Blackroot_Vale_Archers}Arqueros del Valle de la Raíz Negra
{Blackroot_Vale_Archers_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 105%\nAccuracy: Average\nRange: 180 meters\nMissiles: 24\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nBlackroot Vale is a fertile valley situated on the southern side of the White Mountains. Its lush grassland sustained by the Blackroot River is home to many men who acknowledge Gondor's banner as their own. Though the Gondorians of Blackroot Vale are a rustic people far from the more civilized coastlines of Dol Amroth and Pelargir or the high walls of Minas Tirith, they are known across the land as skilled bowmen and are a welcome addition to Gondor's armies. Living so close to the slopes of the White Mountains these archers are used to difficult terrain and due to their life style are quite self-sufficient being able to craft their own arrows and maintain their bows while living off the land and surviving by hunting. As the drums of war begin to echo across the White Mountains the people of Blackroot Vale are wary and are making themselves battle-ready for Gondor shall soon have need of them. \n\nREQUIRES ETHRING, CALEMBEL OR SERELOND

{Lamedon_Clansmen}Hombres de los clanes de Lamedon
{Lamedon_Clansmen_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Impetuous\nMovement Speed: 110%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nLamedon is one of the most populated fiefdoms of Gondor and so it is no surprise that the people of this region hold the defence of their nation in high regard. Despite having a high population, the region is very hilly and is divided into sections by two key rivers; the Ciril and Ringló. Due to the highland nature of their home, many inhabitants live in tight-nit 'clan' like communities that act as satellite settlements surrounding Ethring and Calembel. These clans have developed quite a distinct subculture so when the forces of Lamedon are called to honour, the result is battalions of hardy men marching to war in their clans' distinctive garb. The clansmen eschew heavy armour in favour of freedom of movement; which is no bad thing owing to the maces and shields that these warriors favour in battle! They rely on surprise and swiftness in battle to outmaneuver more heavily armoured and slow-footed foes before crushing helm and head with fierce blows with their bludgeoning maces. \n\nREQUIRES ETHRING, TARNOST OR CALEMBEL 
{Lamedon_Clansmen_descr_short}Hardy, swift-footed warriors from the Gondorian fiefdom of Lamedon, armed with a mace and shield. \n\nREQUIRES ETHRING, TARNOST OR CALEMBEL

{Lamedon_Champions}Campeones de Lamedon
{Lamedon_Champions_descr}Morale: Excellent\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nLamedon, divided many times by the waters of the rivers Ciril and Ringlo, sits in the shadow of the great White Mountains of Gondor. In the summer, secluded and separated from the ongoing war, the people here enjoy relative peace compared to the many that border them. They spend their time tending to their flocks and idling by the streams and tributaries that flow down from the mountains. This peace does not last all year long however, as when winter comes, the ground is covered in a thick layer of snow and strong, icy winds tear through the sparsely populated towns and villages. All manner of beasts, birds and other nameless things descend out of the mountains in search of food and rather than foolishly risking their lives warding off these creatures alone, the men of Lamedon divide themselves into small groups or clans, usually led by a chosen "champion" and take the fight North. Adorned proudly in thick leather and gambeson, passed down from their forefathers, the Clansmen arm themselves with shields and maces, knowing that this is not a task of glory but one of duty and importance. There are some however, who have greater ambitions. Ambitions of war. Ambitions of slaying more than just mountain beasts. These "Champions", as they are known amongst the Lamedon people, are those brave (or foolish) enough to cast down their shields and opt instead for a larger, more traditional two-handed claymore or sword. They live or die by their swordsmanship and as such, have garnered quite a reputation amongst the rank and file of Gondor's armies. Woe unto thee who do not recognize these men for what they truly are, for by the time the tartan is slung, it is likely too late.. \n\nREQUIRES ETHRING, TARNOST OR CALEMBEL
{Lamedon_Champions_descr_short}Elite soldiers led by Lord Angbor, heavily armoured and armed with two-handed swords. \n\nREQUIRES ETHRING, TARNOST OR CALEMBEL

{Ringlo_Pikemen}Piqueros de Ringlo
{Ringlo_Pikemen_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nStanding resolute in the heart of Gondor, the Pikemen of the Ringlo Vale emerge as an unwavering bulwark against the tides of darkness that seek to engulf the realm. Armed with the stalwart combination of long spears and chainmail, they epitomize Gondor's valor and undying spirit.. The Pikemen of the Ringlo Vale are the embodiment of disciplined training and cohesive teamwork. Their formations, honed through relentless practice, stand as awe-inspiring displays of unity. With pikes extended, they craft an almost impervious barrier, a testament to their skill and synergy. Their role as the linchpin of Gondor's defense cannot be overstated. They are the steadfast wall that thwarts enemy charges and stands unwavering against all odds. As the realm's beacon against encroaching darkness, the Pikemen of the Ringlo Vale stand tall, draped in their red and white capes, their iron helmets glinting in the sun. They are more than soldiers, they are the embodiment of Gondor's timeless values, a living testament to the courage, unity, and unwavering commitment that will forever endure in the annals of history.\n\nREQUIRES Ethring, Erech, Calembel OR Tarnost

{Anfalas_Herdsmen}Pastores de Anfalas
{Anfalas_Herdsmen_descr}Morale: Poor\nMorale Response: Poor\nMovement Speed: 110%\nAccuracy: Exceptional\nRange: 130 meters\nMissiles: 24\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nAnfalas, or Langstrand as it's referred to by the people of Gondor, is a coastal region that lies between the rivers Lefnui and Morthond, south of Pinnath Gelin. The history of this land is ancient, bearing the boots of the first Numenoreans that would eventually come to populate the rest of Gondor and it's further frontiers. What was once a bustling coastline of trade and activity has now been reduced to a daily struggle for survival and hope. The Corsairs of Umbar and it's allies mount constant raids on the towns and villages of Anfalas, attacking with such swiftness they give the swarthy men of the coast little time for reprise or defence. With Gondor stretched so thin, Lord Golasgil has little resources to effectively govern his domain and so the protection of the people falls largely to themselves. The farmers and labourers arm themselves with what swords they can find, donning thin cloth tabards with a faded symbol of their once great kingdom. The few too weak to don a sword instead learn to utilize the sling, slung with sharp rocks and stones washed up on the shore many centuries ago.  Burnt by the sun, sea air in their nostrils, hope in their hearts and a sling by their side, they stand ready. EQUIRES Edhellond, Lond Galen, Annúlond OR Tharagrondost
{Anfalas_Herdsmen_descr_short}Untrained and unarmoured hunters equipped with a sling and swords.

{Citadel_Guard}Guardia de la ciudadela
{Citadel_Guard_descr}Morale: Excellent\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nThe Guards of the Citadel, sometimes referred to as the Guards of the Tower of Gondor, were the warriors responsible for guarding the upmost level of Minas Tirith, especially the courtyard that held the fountain and the White Tree of Gondor, and gave way the thronehall.They served as personal bodyguards to the Kings of Gondor, and later to the Stewards of Gondor until King Aragorn II Elessar's coronation. However, with Gondor surrounded on all fronts by new enemies and with old allies facing threats of their own, the Citadel Guard's expertise was neded sorely on the frontlines. As such, the Guard were sent forth to recruit from all of Gondor's towns and cities and to select only the finest soldiers to join them. What was once a ceremonial Guard of the King and his home is now Gondor's most elite fighting force.

{Minas_Ithil_Guard}Guardianes de Minas Ithil
{Minas_Ithil_Guard_descr}Locked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Snow\n\nWith the Morgul Vale cleansed of the foul taint of the Úlari and their servants the Tower of the Moon once again stands ready to protect Gondor from the shadow. Stationed at Minas Ithil are the newly formed Guardians. Recruited from the ranks of veterans that have fought against the hosts of the dark lord, these men have taken a vow to stand vigilant against the shadow and to protect the Tower of the Moon for the awaited return of the king. Armed and armoured with the very best gear from Gondor’s armouries, these soldiers fight in highly disciplined ranks of heavy swordsmen. Proudly wearing tabards that show the new symbol of Minas Ithil – the moon and tree, and ready to lay down their lives in the defence of Gondor; these men will fight to the very end. With the defence of the newly named Minas Ithil an absolute priority, these men have been known to fight under the banner of other forces of good in order to protect the Vale. \n\nREQUIRES MINAS ITHIL

{Eorling_Spearmen_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nWhat is a Rider of Rohan without his horse? An excellent spearman that can't gallop, that's for sure. These Spearmen of Rohan are courageous warriors, and without their normal mounts they are only encouraged to fight harder! Fit with ordinary scale-mail and wielding a fine spear and round shield, the greatest weapon these Spearmen possess is their ability to unseat other horsemen with calm and cool precision and efficiency. Rohirrim translated from Sindarin means "Horse-lords," and it is immediately obvious that the people of Rohan are the unparalleled masters of horses. This natural affinity between the Rohirrim and their steeds gives them a unique and deadly set of battle tactics, for no one knows how to use AND defend against a furious cavalry charge better than the Horse-lords themselves! Rohan Spearmen may not have the best armour or the strongest resolve, but what they do have is a natural ability to deftly wield a spear in many different ways, and expertly impale an Uruk or a Warg in many different places. 
{Eorling_Spearmen_descr_short}Light spearmen with good morale and above average mobility, they are adept at defending against both horse and infantry charges.

{Breeland_Bodyguards}Guardia del consejo
{Breeland_Bodyguards_descr}Morale: Very Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nGeneral's Unit\nTerrain bonus: Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nOnce called the North-South Road, Greenway is a remnant of an immense highway that connected the great kingdoms of Arnor and Gondor. After the fall of Arnor the road became neglected and much of it fell into disuse with the northern section becoming overgrown earning it the name Greenway. Despite its poor condition the Greenway remains Eriador's best line of contact and trade with other kingdoms. As such it is an important strategic resource and whatever skilled and well equipped men the Human townships of Eriador can muster are sent into the ranks of the aptly named Greenway Guards. Not only do they patrol the road and keep it open but by operating in the wilderness the Greenway Guards are the first line of defence that keeps many of the dangers away from the vulnerable settlements. From this group of hardy guardians come the Bree-land Bodyguards. After having served a minimum of 2 years on the frontlines, the best of the Guards are taken to defend the ruling Council of Bree. In so doing, they have become the best warriors that Bree can field, though not as strong as the Dúnedain, they will still fight valiantly for the land they love. 
{Breeland_Bodyguards_descr_short}Greenway Guards that have proven their strength and are chosen to defend the ruling council of Bree.

{Greenway_Cavalry_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nSecondary Attack: 4\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Forest, Snow\n\nOnce called the North-South Road, Greenway is a remnant of an immense highway that connected the great kingdoms of Arnor and Gondor. After the fall of Arnor the road became neglected and much of it fell into disuse with the northern section becoming overgrown earning it the name Greenway. Despite its poor condition the Greenway remains Eriador's best line of contact and trade with other kingdoms. As such it is an important strategic resource and whatever skilled and well equipped men the Human townships of Eriador can muster are sent into the ranks of the aptly named Greenway Guards. Not only do they patrol the road and keep it open but by operating in the wilderness the Greenway Guards are the first line of defence that keep many of the dangers away from the vulnerable settlements. The Guard themselves are split into two regiments: the Riders who patrol the road on horseback and aid beleaguered travellers, and the Spearmen who maintain checkpoints at specific sections along the road that are of special importance; such as bridges, road-side inns and so on. In times of greater danger the mounted and foot regiments come together to form a formidable wall of battle. 
{Greenway_Cavalry_descr_short}Mounted guardians of the Greenway oftentimes found patrolling along the road.

{Watchmen_Sword_Regiment}Espadachines de la Guardia de Vigilantes
{Watchmen_Sword_Regiment_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nThe lands surrounding Bree do not have a formal military, however, they do have cohorts of men who defend the Bree-land from any danger. These men most commonly fight in the ranks of Bree-land Militia but if a division gains a wealth of experience and proves its loyalty to the Mayor of Bree, it can become elevated to to the status of Watchmen; the closest thing that Bree has to a formal military. Bree-land militia are just that, a militia unit, drafted in times of need, they tend not to venture very far from Bree and rarely are seen in the territories frequented by the Dúnedain. The Watchmen are different. They are experienced militia who are given a greater degree of protection, but this comes with a price. These cohorts patrol the very borders of the Bree-land, away from their homes and families. If the Dúnedain cannot turn or route an invasion then the Watchmen are ready; ever vigilant and willing to die to protect the peaceful Bree-land. The swordsmen are the defensive division of the Watchmen, armed with strong swords and wearing improved armour over their brother cohorts, inspired by the men of Dale. With their strong Bree-land shields they are a good medium force who excel at holding back charges and lasting in the frantic maelstrom of melee combat. 
{Watchmen_Sword_Regiment_descr_short}Stout swordsmen from the troubled borders of the Bree-land.

¬######### NORTHERN DÚNEDAIN
{Dunedain_Bodyguards}Guardaespaldas dúnedain
{Dunedain_Bodyguards_descr}Locked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 105%\nGeneral's Unit\nSecondary effective against armour\nAccuracy: Very High\nRange: 230 meters\nMissiles: 34\nArmour Piercing Missiles\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nThese men represent the veteran rangers who have mastered the manufacturing and handling of the famed Númenórean steelbows after the technological and cultural changes brought about by the reforging of Arnor. Men used to long periods of time in the woods and facing all kinds of perilous creatures, including the resilient Trolls, their weapon of choice is a two handed axe that is swung with great might. These powerful Rangers are quite used to individual heroics and have chosen a new role and tactics over fitting into the old ways of Arnor’s formation based, military doctrine, so becoming powerful flankers but vulnerable to missiles and cavalry charges. Emphasising mobility and skilful attacks over armour the Beastslayers operate best out of ambush in wooded areas or in combination with heavy line infantry to distract or pin down the enemy though their fighting prowess makes them potent in most situations. They carry large black-shafted axes, somewhat more elegant than those a lumberjack would use, with one curved axe. They also carry the steel bow, a weapon as hard to draw for most men as longbow would be for a child. This powerful bow is little affected by changes in temperature or humidity and provides superior accuracy and draw weight which coupled with the heavy steel tipped arrows used by the Beastslayers provides it with extraordinary penetration. Left unchecked these marksmen will wreak havoc on the enemy's toughest troops but be sure to choose your targets and positioning wisely.
{Dunedain_Bodyguards_descr_short}Elite ranger marksmen equipped for taking out large and heavily armoured targets.

The secret to creating this fierce weapon has been passed on, from father to son, amongst the smiths of the Dúnedain of the North and now that they have the resources and freedom to work again, they bring their hidden weapon to bear on the world! Similar in design to a standard longbow but with steel limbs, the Steelbow requires monumental force to draw and it is for this reason that its use is rarely seen in the Third Age. The Númenóreans were stronger and taller than the men of the mainland and they could draw these monsters with ease; should an Orc stumble across one of these bows, he would likely tear his limbs off before the bow gave way. The men chosen to wield these behemoths can trace their lineage back beyond the days of Arvedui and are perhaps the proudest of all the Dúnedain. Proud, strong, tall and more skilled than any men who walk the earth, these men can rival the accuracy and range of the Elves in combat! Alas, time whithers all things and Númenor was not spared the harsh grip of time. Their nation fell to vanity and greed and was lost beneath the seas. However: the blood of Númenor lived on and now, in the waning years of the Third Age, there are those amongst the united nations of Gondor and old Arnor that hold their blood true and pure. These Sons of Númenor have been chosen amongst all the many souls in the Reunited Kingdom to guard over that which the Dúnedain hold most dear: the chief cities of Minas Tirith and Annúminas. An honour guard who rarely sees duty, they are nevertheless the kingdom's finest warriors and their wall of halberds will ensure nothing: no shadow or invader will ever again defile the halls of the King. \n\nREQUIRES ANNUMINAS.

{Royal_Guardsmen_descr}Morale: Very Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nGeneral's Unit\nAccuracy: High\nRange: 170 meters\nMissiles: 24\nArmour Piercing Missiles\nTerrain bonus: Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nBefore Dale was burned by Smaug the Golden, an elite force of archers guarded the city, wielding both bow and spear to defend the Palace of Girion and the king who lived within. After Bard reclaimed the city and slew Smaug, he sought to recreate this force so the generals of Dale would be guarded by capable men; using spears once meant for the Dorwinion King, Bladorthin, Bard equipped his best men. However, despite the abundance of plate armour Bard declined it, instead choosing to garb the archers in chainmail, not wishing to clothe a select few in rich garments while the rest suffered; instead the plate was melted down for tools or sold to fund Dale’s reconstruction. \n\nRegardless of their origin, these men who are garbed in the red of the Girion’s Palace are a force to be feared for they are fine archers that can pick off enemy troops easily, before turning to dispose of any remaining enemy cavalry or infantry with their spears. Any who face Dale in combat would do well to look past the humble garments of these men, for to underestimate them is to risk death.

Dale has suffered the wrath of a Dragon twice before, and they intend never to endure that fate again. To this end, the kings of Dale turned to their allies in Mirkwood and Erebor and created this corps. Dwarf and Elf crafted plated armour, spears, shields, and bows, and trained the best warriors in Dale in their use. To this panoply, the men of Dale added their fearsome Black Arrows. Made after the pattern of the arrow that pierced the great wyrm Smaug's breast, these heavy bolts will make a mockery of any armour worn by a lesser creature. Taking the name Hearthguard, these men have sworn that the homes of the Dalefolk will not know Dragonfire again. Whether that Dragon comes as a great tide of Rhunnic zealots or as a winged wyrm, the Hearthguard will slay the beast and defend their people. \n\nREQUIRES DALE OR ESGAROTH

{Greenwood_Foresters_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 110%\nCan place stakes\nAccuracy: Very High\nRange: 220 meters\nMissiles: 32\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nThe borders of Mirkwood are home to a strange and forgotten folk who are often simply called the ‘woodsmen’. They are relatives of the Beornings and can trace their ancestry back to the days of the princes of Rhovanion and to the Edain of old. Their mostly blonde hair and tall stature has convinced the lore masters of the Upper Vales that the Woodmen are relatives of the House of Hador. Whatever their history, they are a rough folk who have endured the terror of Dol Guldur for many years. Their constant battle against the shadow has made them secretive and distrustful and they have taken to ambushing any who step foot under the boughs of the Greatwood. \n\nThe men of the wood have long practiced with the bow as a means for food and protection but their dedication to the weapon has birthed a troop of outstanding archers known only as 'The Foresters’. With keen sight, formidable accuracy and strong longbows; crafted with the skill of the Éothéod, these men are excellent killers. Like all woodsmen they deeply love their home but they often spend long periods away; tracking the foul creatures of the Mountains and Dol Guldur and, where possible, ambushing the enemy unawares.
{Greenwood_Foresters_descr_short}Veteran Woodmen warriors from Mirkwood that ambush their foes with longbows and spears.

{Silvan_Marchwardens}Mariscales silvanos
{Silvan_Marchwardens_descr}Morale: Excellent\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 120%\nAccuracy: Very High\nRange: 225 meters\nMissiles: 28\nTerrain bonus: Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nThe Silvan Marchwardens are ambushers matched by none in terms of stealth, secrecy and camouflage. They are an elite bowmen unit and are far stealthier than any archer in Beorn's army. They carry quality longbows with sharp, poisoned arrows, and if necessary they can draw their knives for close-combat. If there is one disadvantage that the Mirkwood Marchwardens have, it is the fact that they are not protected by heavy armour; they are only equipped with light leather armour. This does, however, make them manoeuvrable troops, but it does not shield them as well from danger as heavier armour would have. They are supremely suited to guerrilla warfare and should not be expected to perform well in the open field.\n\nTheir tasks often take them far away from the borders of Mirkwood, further than anyone from Thranduil's army has ever been; for it is their duty to ensure the safety of the Woodland realm and to keep at bay any invading army that intends to enter Mirkwood.\n\nWhen confronted by an overwhelming number of enemies, the Mirkwood Marchwardens will sometimes join forces with the Woodmen who live on the borders of Mirkwood. Together they have succeeded in keeping the hosts of Dol Guldur and those of the Misty Mountains at bay for centuries. \n\nREQUIRES AMON LANC

Sauron's hatred of the Dwarves who dwelled in Khazad-dûm and their Elven allies caused the Misty Mountains to become infested with Orcs bringing the Dwarven stronghold under almost perpetual siege and plunging it into an uncertain fate. {Kings_Halberds}Alabardas del rey
{Kings_Halberds_descr}Against men and Elves, Dwarves are particularly disadvantaged by their short stature. To remedy this shortcoming, some Dwarven warriors have adopted the halberd, the crude -- though effective -- combination of an axe head and a long pole. In orderly formations, halberds allow Dwarves to hold enemies off at range. However, for combat in close quarters, a Dwarven halberdier will often switch to a double-bladed axe in order to finish off his enemies. The King's Regiments form the backbone of the unshakeable Dwarven army. 
{Kings_Halberds_descr_short}Warriors armed with halberds and axes. Able to form a defensive wall.

In reverance to their honoured dead, Dwarves from Ered Luin and Erebor, newly settled in the Mountains, have formed into ranks of formidable spear men; wearing the same armour of old and wielding mighty spears, they take to the battlefield in sturdy formations under the name 'Sons of the Fallen'. {Blacklock_Engineers}Ingenieros morenos
{Blacklock_Engineers_descr}Morale: Excellent\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 95%\nCan place stakes\nSecondary effective against armour\nAccuracy: High\nRange: 160 meters\nMissiles: 26\nArmour Piercing Missiles\nTerrain bonus: Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nDwarves are famous for the quality of their war machines, a fact they delight in. Few cities can stand against the creations of the Dwarves and there are no greater creations than those made in the far off Orocarni by the Blacklock clan. Their war machines have been known to level gates and walls in a single well aimed shot. However, possibly their most prized invention is the superior crossbow. Many Dwarves prefer crossbows to bows as they are simple, deadly and require little practice allowing more time for mining! The Blacklocks have taken the standard crossbow and improved it greatly but the weaknesses of the crossbow remain: its long reload time over a bow. This has lead the Blacklock Crossbowmen to adopt very heavy armour in order to ensure they can fight off any melee threats before releasing a deadly volley upon their foes once more.\n\nContrary to popular belief, the Dwarves do not always shy away from war and the troubles of others. In truth, if their kin are threatened then the Dwarves have been known to rally together in astounding numbers and crush their enemies. The last occurence of which was the Dwarven retaliation to the killing of King Thrór in T.A 2790, whereby the Dwarves showed the world that they were a force to be reckoned with in the 'War of the Dwarves and Orcs'. The war called upon all of the seven families of the Dwarves, and all answered the call. Now, as then, the Dwarves of Erebor are threatened by the vastness of Rhûn and the plague like spread of the shadow of Mordor. They have called for aid from the four clans of the Orocarni and the call has been answered!

{Azaghals_Tomb_Protectors}Protectores de la tumba de Azaghâl
{Azaghals_Tomb_Protectors_descr}Locked Morale - Can't be broken\nMovement Speed: 95%\nTerrain bonus: Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nDwarves have a long memory. Longer than any of the races of Middle-earth. And many of them remember very well the grievous Nirnaeth Arnoediad, and fiery wrath of Glaurung, and the glory of fallen king of Belegost, crushed by the Great Worm. Ever since that day Dwarves of Ered-Luin gather every year to sing an ancient dirge in memory of their great ancestor. Azaghal's Tomb Protectors are the descendants of the warriors of the past, who carried the body of Azaghal from the field of battle to his eternal resting place. They keep ward over the magnificent vault built on the highest peak of the Blue Mountains. To perform this duty they equip themselves with a powerful two-handed axe, which requires great strength to wield, while they are clad in both mail and plate armour for protection. They also wear a heavy steel helm which covers their face like a mask, thus clearly showing their pride to hail from the Ered Luin; the Blue Mountains. Their fury is unmatched on the battlefield, as they sweep through the ranks of the enemy, leaving no survivor. On the hill's summit there are two circles of trees in whose branches King Amroth, the previous ruler of Lothlórien, built his residence. King Amroth and his house are long gone but the hill retains its great importance to the Galadhrim. Sentinels of Cerin Amroth are an honour guard who keep watch over this sacred place and ensure that its beauty remains unspoiled. From the topmost flet in the inner circle of trees, which was once the highest point in all of Lothlórien, the Sentinels observe the surrounding lands and are even able to keep watch on Dol Guldur itself. Just as the flowers that grow on the hill, elanor and niphredil, are coloured gold and white and the outer trees have white bark while the inner circle are the golden Mallorn trees so the shields of the Sentinels bear those two colours as well, symbolizing the things it is their duty to protect. All Sentinels are expected to uphold the legacy of King Amroth, beauty, prosperity, valour and wisdom, and lend their minds or swords, whichever is needed, to the protection or betterment of the Goldenwood. With war looming ever greater on the fringes of Lorien the Sentinels are being deployed further and further from the Hill and now have the name in a merely ceremonial fashion. {High_Paladins}Altos paladines
{High_Paladins_descr}Morale: Excellent\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 105%\nGeneral's Unit\nTerrain bonus: Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nDorwinion's wealth and power comes from its vast vinyards and excellent wine, however due to its remote and dangerous location it was necessary to develop a professional and elite fighting force to protect Dorwinion from continuous raids, especially as the many mercenaries of the land were fickle and greedy. The Court of Dorwinion and the Vine-Regent also needed a dependable and loyal protective force as their growing wealth made them targets of assassins and corrupt nobles. This gave birth to the High Paladins of the Vintner Court. This fierce unit is made up of both men and elves hand-picked by the Vine-Regent himself, with selection to this battalion being considered a great honor; those selected are deemed the ‘True Sons’ of Dorwinion. Decades – or in some cases centuries – of defending their homeland from Rhûn have left these soldiers with a battle-hardened spirit and an unwavering loyalty to their court. Throughout their battles, the Paladins have honed their skills with their heavy two-handed swords, and, like the Easterlings, they don strong golden armour. The High Paladins make up the elite core of the Dorwinion army and they can turn the tide of many battles with their long blades, and their will to defend their liege lord.
{High_Paladins_descr_short}Well armoured Dorwinion Knights serving as Bodyguards.

Although as a people the Dorwinrim are not renowned as warriors these spearmen are still quite capable of driving away raiders and bandits from their lords' lands. In wartime these soldiers form a mainstay force that the Lords of Dorwinion can rely on to fight with a partisan determination. Armed with spears and stout shields, these warriors are relatively well drilled in fighting in close support of one another, relying on their lamellar coats to turn aside arrow or blade. These men will be hard pressed fighting against heavy infantry or cavalry and the Lords of Dorwinion must depend on more heavily armed mercenaries to counter such forces.

Heavily armoured Thorn soldiers lacked the training and discipline required to track down Rhûnic patrols and raiding parties over long distances. Many slipped through the vast border and burned Dorwinion’s fertile vineyards. The various vintners and lords of Dorwinion sought to combat this, and as such the Dorwinion Swordmasters were born. The Swordmasters wear much lighter armour so they can keep up their stamina longer and swiftly traverse the vast plains of Dorwinion. The Avari elves have proven themselves to be masters of battle tactics and sword fighting techniques, skills they have passed to the men of Dorwinion. Now they fight side-by-side as the shock troops of Dorwinion, their two-handed claymores cleaving through those that stand against them. Lightly armoured units stand little chance against the Swordmasters, with their only true foe being the heavily armoured Lôke units of Rhûn. Now the vineyards of Dorwinion can lie secure knowing that the Swordmasters are out on the front lines. \n\nREQUIRES DORWINION HOMELANDS

{Avari_Warriors}Guerreros avari
{Avari_Warriors_descr}Morale: Very Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 110%\nTerrain bonus: Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nUnlike the mercenaries of the region, who are used to defend the wealthiest barons and lords, the Elves fight for honour and do not covet wealth. Instead they fight to keep the nations trade routes open. If a caravan enters into Dorwinion, they will not know it but they are already under the eyes of the Avari. They do not act as escorts but instead spring into action when a caravan or road is threatened. This is not out of desire to protect the wealthy of Dorwinion but instead to protect their homes as raids are so frequent in Dorwinion that the Elves needed a way to protect themselves. The Avari High Council created the Avari Warriors to act as border guards; descending on unsuspecting enemies to overwhelm them and then disappear back into the countryside. To aid in their hit and run tactics, they wear reinforced leather armour and opt to use simple yet effective swords and shields. They are so elusive that even the Human lords do not even know their true number and like all Elves they are well disciplined and fight to the death almost invariably.

Since then, a permanent garrison of Crossbowmen at Caras Sant has protected the castle, using both iron and steel crossbows. By nature, a crossbow is both slow and deadly, both an advantage and a burden. Although crossbows aren't the most common of weapons used in Dorwinion, the smiths are always ready to make more of such a powerful weapon after their tremendous success against the hordes of Rhûn. The crossbow excelled at piercing the armour of the Easterlings and in the 'Battle of the Southern Gardens' a small force of Crossbowmen brought down 2 regiments of the famous Kataphract cavalry, forcing a mass retreat by the Rhûnnic general and a glorious victory for Dorwinion. These stalwart and precise warriors take their name from the many gardens of Dorwinion and, in particular, the gardens of the High Court of Dorwinion wherein the two councils of Dorwinion sit and the Vine-Regent holds his court but most importantly, many roses grow. They have sworn to defend the beauty of their flowered homeland.

{Court_Knights}Caballeros de la corte viticultora
{Court_Knights_descr}Morale: Excellent\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nCan place stakes\nTerrain bonus: Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nDorwinion's wealth and power comes from its vast vinyards and excellent wine, however due to its remote and dangerous location it was necessary to develop a professional fighting force to protect Dorwinion from continuous raids, especially as the many mercenaries of the land were fickle and greedy. The Court of Dorwinion and the Vine-Regent also needed a dependable and loyal protective force as their growing wealth made them targets of assassins and corrupt nobles. This gave birth to the Knights of the Vintner Court. Taken from the ranks of 'true sons' of Dorwinion and mostly from the working class, they have a strong sense of loyalty to their homeland and this gives them a ferocity on the battlefield. Decades of war with Rhûn have left these soldiers battle scarred and ready for any situation but has also helped to develop their equipment; like the Easterlings, these men don strong golden armour and wield short swords. Their proud and strong armour became renowned throughout the lands of Rhovanion and men came to fear the sight of the golden warriors. As such, the lord-like Earls of Dale sought to wear the same armour for themselves, they donned the armour as best they could but due to the expensive nature of the armour, they could only wear a steel replica; the Lords of Dale and the members of the ruling family still ride to war in the Dorwinion golden Knight armour though. They are an elite unit and as such are not very numerous but often just their presence on the battlefield leads to a Dorwinion victory.

This gave birth to the Paladins of the Vintner Court: the Knights mounted counterpart. Taken from the ranks of 'true sons' of Dorwinion and mostly from the working class, they have a strong sense of loyalty to their homeland and this gives them a ferocity on the battlefield. Decades of war with Rhûn have left these soldiers battle scarred and ready for any situation and, perhaps most importantly, has helped to develop their equipment. Like the Easterlings, these men don strong golden armour and wield short swords. Their proud and strong armour became renowned throughout the lands of Rhovanion and men came to fear the sight of the golden warriors. As such, the lord-like Earls of Dale sought to wear the same armour for themselves, they donned the armour as best they could but due to the expensive nature of the armour, they could only wear a steel replica; the Lords of Dale and the members of the ruling family still ride to war in the Dorwinion golden Knight armour though. The Paladins make up the elite core of the Dorwinion cavalry regiments and they can turn the tide of many battles with their strong mounts. 
{Court_Paladins_descr_short}Heavily armoured cavalry with a sword and shield.

As such, he has equipped some of the finest men of the levy battalions in a heavier armour and put them through rigorous training and the outcome are the warriors dubbed 'The Regent's Finest'. Taking to the field in formations of spearmen, heavy axemen and crossbowmen, they will defend Dorwinion to their last breath and swear undying loyalty to the Vine-Regent. ¬######### ENEDWAITH
{Clan_Heralds}Heraldos del clan
{Clan_Heralds_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 110%\nGeneral's Unit\nAccuracy: Average\nRange: 70 meters\nMissiles: 4\nArmour Piercing Missiles\nTerrain bonus: Forest\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nOnce, chieftains were guarded by their personal retinues chosen from among the most capable and trusted men of their clan. However, with the decline of the clans there is not only an increasingly lack of such men but also a disturbing increase in the stories of such men backstabbing their own chieftains to claim their mantles. To remedy this, the Clansmoot formed the order of Clan Heralds. Holding grand melees to find the most capable warriors, they are then sworn to the Clansmoot rather than any one clan and are assigned to chieftains in need of such bodyguards. Stationed away from their own clans does much to discourage treachery and power grabbing.\n\nBold and strong, these men carry javelins and two-handed blades into war. Having both great skills, and the highest quality equipment the clans can provide allows them to match many of their foes. They should not be underestimated for they are among the most capable warriors that the Clans of Enedwaith can field.  
{Clan_Heralds_descr_short}Well trained and experienced, these men are formidable warriors and determined leaders.

{Mochaini_Touta}Touta mocháini
{Mochaini_Touta_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 110%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nThe Mocháini are the educated nobles and diplomats of Enedwaith, with their Clanseat standing proudly in Alfcud itself. Long have they served as wardens of the city and the Clanmoot, standing firm against foes from both within and without. Almost all of the Mocháini are capable axe wielders, and it is this weapon that serves as their emblem, showing both their might on the battlefield but also their skill with such tools in harvesting lumber to construct their holds.\n\nThe Touta are the militia levy of the Mocháini, being common men whom have answered the call to arms in such dire times. Although their talents may lie in skill, lore and craft, these men have taken up axes to defend both the Clansmoot and their homeland, and march into war. While they are spirited, they wield only simple axes and shields and wear only simple tabards, and so they are unlikely to be the match for trained soldiers. Nevertheless, these men will do their best to defend their homes. 
{Mochaini_Touta_descr_short}Lightly armed axemen of Clan Mocháini.

{Fylani_Herders}Pastores fylani
{Fylani_Herders_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nAccuracy: Average\nRange: 70 meters\nMissiles: 6\nArmour Piercing Missiles\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Forest, Snow\n\nOnce the Fylani were nomads whom roamed the southern plains, in the shadow of the White Mountains. However, some three hundred years past, the Dunlending Warlord Wulf led a ruthless conquest against Rohan, and he singled out the clan for their kinship with the Clansmoot whom had sheltered rebels from Dunland. Offering cavalry to the Clansmoot, the Fylani were granted lands and refuge in the north. Now, they dwell among the deforested plains of the Great Forest.\n\nThese men are herders whom have answered the call to arms. Although they may lack formal training, they are among the few cavalry the Clans can field, and so should not be disregarded lightly. Their speed allows them to chase down fleeing foes, as they herd their own flocks. Nevertheless, they are still levies, and should not be expected to hold their own against formal soldiers. 
{Fylani_Herders_descr_short}Mounted tribesmen of Clan Fylani, these men make effective mounted skirmishers.

{Keefei_Huntsmen}Cazadores keefei
{Keefei_Huntsmen_descr}Morale: Poor\nMorale Response: Poor\nMovement Speed: 110%\nAccuracy: Average\nRange: 170 meters\nMissiles: 24\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nTo speak of the Keefei is to speak of the decline of Enedwaith itself. Once, according to legend, the Keefei were one of the ruling clans of Enedwaith in the Time of the Great Forest. Then, the Númenóreans attacked. Seeing the clans as both kin to the distrusted Second House of the Edain and subhuman men of the shadow, they crushed them as insects and the Great Forest came to ruin. The Keefei, once noble, fled their homes to the south and found refuge with their kin, the Drúedain. Their emblem of a noble deer speaks of this faded glory.\n\nNow, they are mere hunters who have taken up arms to defend the clans. Although they may not have military training, they are among the very few in the clans who are skilled in the bow and have been taught stealth and hunting by their kin of the Drúadan Forest. These unique skills make them an indispensable component of the armies of the Clanmoot even though they may not be professional soldiers.

{Enedwaith_Cavalry}Caballería de Enedwaith
{Enedwaith_Cavalry_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Forest, Snow\n\nWith the Declaration of Athámor, the time came for the Clans of Enedwaith to develop a centralized military, under the authority of their Ruling Council of Athámor, as an answer to the legions commanded by their neighbours. Acting as an extension of the existing Clan Heralds, this force does not restrict itself by clan, and enlists all with a love for the clans’ new nation. United under the Knot of Athámor and garbed in matching uniforms and sturdy armour, they are a professional force to be reckoned with.\n\nThese mounted lancers number among this force, acting as a capable mounted force, having been trained by the Fylani Clan. Enedwaith has long lacked an elite cavalry force, relying on either mounted shepherds or war wagons, and so has filled a role that the scattered levy system lacked. Like the shepherds, they are skilled at running down routing forces, but unlike them, they are fierce warriors capable of charges into enemy lines.

Made of stern stuff, these men are used to a hard life and know what it is to have nothing and are willing to defend their homelands. Skilled in using javelins for hunting fish, they now have turned this skill to hunting men, and their javelins should be feared.

However, unlike the Fylani whom already held ties with the Clansmoot, the Faolan were originally its foe, for they were of Dunland itself. Rebelling against Wulf’s savage consolidation of power that eroded the ties of clan, they turned cloak and swore an oath to the Clansmoot. In return for guarding the borders of Enedwaith against Dunland with their lives, they were granted a formal seat on it. Men of the Faolan retain many old traditions of Dunland, such as heavy drinking and fighting with almost no armour or clothing but are the fiercest warriors of all the clans.\n\nThese men are stout warders of the borders and stand guard over field and glen with their spears and shields. Although they are of little offensive use, they are among the few among the clans whom can hold the line in battle, and so this sets them apart from their fellow levies.

¬######### DUNLAND
{Chieftains_Bodyguard}Guardia brenin
{Chieftains_Bodyguard_descr}Morale: Very Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nGeneral's Unit\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nLegends speak that the very first Brenin of Dunland was guided to the land by a flock of Crebain that showed him the way, and he established Byrig around the tree that they roosted in. As his sons came of age, they founded their own clans, establishing new villages in the surrounding foothills, and overtime Talon and Clan arose. Dunland was divided into three Talons, the woodlands, the foothills and the plains, with every true Dunlending belonging to both a clan and a Talon, with their brenin ensuring that their clan remains prosperous. These men, adorned with the sacred feathers of the Crebain, ensure that their brenin does not perish in battle. Normally they would bear the patron animal of their clan on their shields, but when they march in service to Dunland's King, they instead bear the Crebain, showing they march for the good of Dunland rather than personal glory or plunder for their clan.\n\nThere can be no doubt these men are the most elite and experienced troops available to a brenin. Having proven their valour in endless years of conflicts, they are handpicked by their brenin himself to serve as their escorts. Clad in sturdy chainmail hauberks and equipped with finely forged swords and reinforced shields, they march into battle with a single purpose- to fulfill their oath sworn to their master. By protecting him with their own lives, regardless of the situation and the odds of survival, they ensure that their own clan remains strong and prosperous, guided by his leadership and thus, with their conviction and purpose firm, they will neither bend nor break before any mortal foe.
{Chieftains_Bodyguard_descr_short}Elite Dunlending warriors, equipped with a one handed sword and a reinforced shield serving their Brenin as bodyguards.
¬-----
{Clan_Spearmen}Lanceros del clan
{Clan_Spearmen_descr}Morale: Poor\nMorale Response: Poor\nMovement Speed: 110%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nDunland is home to a myriad of smaller clans and tribes, lands of quickly assembled war-bands and shifting alliances. These men are from those places, from a bend of the river there, or a wood here. Shepherds, hunters, farmers, fishers - clanfolk. Armed with whatever they had at hand, from the tools of the shepherd to stone-clubs, these men of all ages serve as the bulk of forces in warbands led by their brenins- when not at war, they often assist with farming, hunting or the garrison of a clan's fortifications. Their commander would do well to remember that these are not true men of war and their courage is fickle and best spent in a single furious charge - with the real fighting left to the actual warriors, closing in behind them.\n\nWhen their homes are in danger and their villages in need, these farmers and herdsmen equip themselves with spears and round wooden shields, forming an irregular and untrained army, serving the purpose of deploying great numbers rather than a challenging military force. They are not here to fight for glory, and will often flee rather than falling in battle, for they rarely fight for glory, and are often only motivated by necessity. They can act to hold the line, but have little protection of their own, often wearing little more than their cloaks. Do not expect them to sacrifice their lives and a commander who orders them to charge into the ranks of enemies is most unwise. 
{Clan_Spearmen_descr_short}Untrained and unarmoured peasants equipped with a spear and a wooden round shield.

{Clan_Axemen}Hacheros del clan
{Clan_Axemen_descr}Morale: Poor\nMorale Response: Poor\nMovement Speed: 110%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nDunland is home to a myriad of smaller clans and tribes, lands of quickly assembled war-bands and shifting alliances. These men are from those places, from a bend of the river there, or a wood here. Shepherds, hunters, farmers, fishers - clanfolk. Armed with whatever they had at hand, from the tools of the shepherd to stone-clubs, these men of all ages serve as the bulk of forces in warbands led by their brenins- when not at war, they often assist with farming, hunting or the garrison of a clan's fortifications. Their commander would do well to remember that these are not true men of war and their courage is fickle and best spent in a single furious charge - with the real fighting left to the actual warriors, closing in behind them.\n\nOriginating from the poor villages of the barren-hills of Dunland, these hunters and farmers form the backbone of Dunlending warbands. Raiding parties clad in their cloaks and equipped with axes and wooden shields, these brave warriors are the first to be called to arms at any given time. They are often used to weaken the enemy's ranks just before their commander commands a full frontal assault, while they also show great potential when attacking or flanking disorganised enemy lines. However, while they are braver and more ferocious than their kin who wield spears, they are still farmers and hunters, and value their own lives more than any glory or loot won in battle. 
{Clan_Axemen_descr_short}Untrained and unarmoured peasants equipped with an axe and a wooden round shield.

{Clan_Hunters}Cazadores del clan
{Clan_Hunters_descr}Morale: Poor\nMorale Response: Poor\nMovement Speed: 110%\nAccuracy: Exceptional\nRange: 130 meters\nMissiles: 18\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nDunland is home to a myriad of smaller clans and tribes, lands of quickly assembled war-bands and shifting alliances. These men are from those places, from a bend of the river there, or a wood here. Shepherds, hunters, farmers, fishers - clanfolk. Armed with whatever they had at hand, from the tools of the shepherd to stone-clubs, these men of all ages serve as the bulk of forces in warbands led by their brenins- when not at war, they often assist with farming, hunting or the garrison of a clan's fortifications. Their commander would do well to remember that these are not true men of war and their courage is fickle and best spent in a single furious charge - with the real fighting left to the actual warriors, closing in behind them.\n\nThe life of a hunter was never an easy one. This band of irregulars consists of the very ones that provide the necessary food supplies to their clan. However, they are also often called by necessity to abandon their hunting lodge and offer their services to their brenin, turning their slings to hunting men instead. Hardened but poorly equipped with little more than their cloaks, slings and hatchets, the stones fired from their slings are better suited to hunting animals, but should not be understimated for their ability to slay lightly armored foes. However, they are not warriors and will quickly flee if the battle turns against them, and while they have their hunting hatchets, they are poorly suited to melee and will quickly fall or flee. Nevertheless, when used well, they can hunt men just as easily as they do beasts. 
{Clan_Hunters_descr_short}Untrained and unarmoured hunters equipped with an sling and a hatchet, with some limited close combat abilities.

{Beast_Slayers}Matadores dunhird
{Beast_Slayers_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Impetuous\nMovement Speed: 105%\nAccuracy: Average\nRange: 220 meters\nMissiles: 28\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nDunhird- a single world with multiple meanings. There exists no easy translation, for it can refer to Dunlending culture, religion, miltary tactics, or even something as broad as their very way of life and sustance practises. However, in more recent times it as become a label specifically for traditional Dunlending culture, that which existed before Wulf's grand reforms. And hence, warriors that continue to follow these beliefs, despite Wulf's reforms have become known as the Dunhird. They remain true to Dunland's traditional adversion to using beasts in combat and refuse to use mounts, and are immensly skilled in hunting both men and beast.\n\nThose who embrace these traditons deeply and with all their heart become Dunhird Slayers, representing the pinnacle of these practises. They alone of Dunland are trained in the art of the bow, using it to hunt all manner of men and beast, protecing their clans from raids of goblins, wargs and trolls. They possess a deep affinity with the land, allowing them to hide almost anywhere and their knowledge of beasts ensures that those they hunt will almost always fall to their arrows. Led by Hunt-Masters who act as spiritual leaders for Dunland, they should be feared for their ability to face certain death and stand strong. 
{Beast_Slayers_descr_short}Group of excellent Dunhird monster slayers with keen bows and sturdy spears.

{Dunlending_Bersekers}Berserkers dunhird
{Dunlending_Bersekers_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Impetuous\nMovement Speed: 114%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nDunhird- a single world with multiple meanings. There exists no easy translation, for it can refer to Dunlending culture, religion, miltary tactics, or even something as broad as their very way of life and sustance practises. However, in more recent times it as become a label specifically for traditional Dunlending culture, that which existed before Wulf's grand reforms. And hence, warriors that continue to follow these beliefs, despite Wulf's reforms have become known as the Dunhird. They remain true to Dunland's traditional adversion to using beasts in combat and refuse to use mounts, and are immensly skilled in hunting both men and beast.\n\nA core foundation of Dunhird beliefs is that grudges should be avenged, and the price of blood must be paid. These men are those who settle them, whether it is merely against enemy clans, or even enemy nations such as the Clans of Enedwaith, or Rohan, whom Dunlendins mock as the Forgil, which means 'strawheads' in the Dunlending tongue, on account of their blond hair. Often youths are tasked with this duty as an initiation rite, but there are also many men who chose this as their life, however short it may be. The runes painted upon their chests have sacred meanings, recording the wrongs they are avenging and invoking the spirits of Dunland to support them. Hateful and ferocious, these men wield clubs and small shields, using their rage to avenge past wrongs, screaming wildly into battle. 
{Dunlending_Bersekers_descr_short}Bands of impetuous Dunhird berserkers, who join the battle wielding brutal spiked war clubs and shields.
¬-----
{Dunlending_Raiders}Incursores dunledinos
{Dunlending_Raiders_descr}Morale: Average\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 114%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nA Dunlending Warrior is always eager to prove himself as mighty and worthy in the eyes of his clan, relishing in conflict and battle. Adding to that is their undying hatred for the Rohirrim, whom they considered to have stolen their homeland. Those of the Clan Levy who have shown their skill advance from the levy to become professional soldiers, donning Dunland's traditional armor. Unlike the King's Warband who are stationed in Byrig and serve the Royal Line, these men still serve their own brenin directly. And even if they are less armored than the King's Warband, they are equally skilled and are able to make up for their lighter armor with more savage attacks.\n\nThese men are the quintessential example of Dunlending raiders. In normal times, serving and raiding under their brenin, they bear their clan's patron animal upon their shield. However, when they march in service to the Line of Wulf, they replace it with the symbol of the Crebain, to declare to the world and their fellow clans that they do not march for their own glory, but for the glory of Dunland as a whole. Capable, deadly and skilled, they can charge into their foes, dealing death with their curved swords while using their shields to protect themselves. 
{Dunlending_Raiders_descr_short}Battle-hardened tribesmen equipped with curved swords and shields.
¬-----
{Wulfs_Pikes}Piqueros de la guardia de Wulf
{Wulfs_Pikes_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Impetuous\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nTo face the Wulfguard is said to be akin to facing the Judgment of Wulf himself. Wulf's closest friends and allies were gifted the finest armor and arms, from both his personal stores initially, and then the armories of Rohan. So fine, that Dunland lacked the skill and facilities to reproduce them and can only replace them by looting foreign armories. At Wulf's death, his closest friends and allies swore to protect his line so that one day, they would once again hold Edoras. Passing down their armor and arms as heirlooms, the Wulfingir is an elite force descended from these men, composed of axemen and pikes usually led by the King's right and left hand. It is said their arms and armour were restored and strengthened by the dwarves themselves in recognition of Dunland's aid to them during their long wanderings, which is why they have lasted for two centuries without decaying unlike most arms and armor forged by men.\n\nThese men wield pikes and stand firm, their conviction and faith in the Royal Line of Wulf almost unbreaking. It is said that if they guard a fortress, it will break before they do. Furthermore, with their pikes, they can supremely equipped to match Rohan's forces in open fields, countering their foe's mounted forces. These men should be feared for their skill and ferocity.\n\nREQUIRES LANDMARK BUILDING.  
{Wulfs_Pikes_descr_short}Highly trained elite pikemen, equipped with finely crafted armour and long sharp pikes.\n\nREQUIRES LANDMARK BUILDING.

{Wulfs_Swordsmen}Partida de guerra de espaderos
{Wulfs_Swordsmen_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian with assets Castellan of Angmar*Taurion*\n\nThe most basic unit in a Dunlending military host is that of a brenin's warband- composed of mostly levy troops, some professional warriors and a small elite band of the brenin and their oathbound retainers and bodyguards. Established by Wulf, the King's Warband is an extension of this concept on the scale of a nation- instead of serving a brenin and their clan, this Warband serves Dunland and its King. Stationed in Byrig and garbed in some of the finest arms and armour that Dunland can produce, using Rohirric forging techniques, the Warband has been mocked by some for relying on their armor over their skill, but nevertheless, they are a force to be feared.\n\nThese particular men are trained in the use of swords and shields. Many are descended from the Rohirric companions of Wulf who settled in Byrig, and are loyal to Wulf's line. Steadfast in their conviction, they are dependable warriors willing to die for their ruler. However, their armour weighs them down, and they lack the offensive skill of traditional Dunlending warriors. 
{Wulfs_Swordsmen_descr_short}Elite heavily armoured sword-wielding wildmen infantry loyal to Wulf's line, clad in lamellar and holding a shield.

{Dunlending_Warband}Partida de guerra de lanceros
{Dunlending_Warband_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian with assets Castellan of Angmar*Taurion*\n\nThe most basic unit in a Dunlending military host is that of a brenin's warband- composed of mostly levy troops, some professional warriors and a small elite band of the brenin and their oathbound retainers and bodyguards. Established by Wulf, the King's Warband is an extension of this concept on the scale of a nation- instead of serving a brenin and their clan, this Warband serves Dunland and its King. Stationed in Byrig and garbed in some of the finest arms and armour that Dunland can produce, using Rohirric forging techniques, the Warband has been mocked by some for relying on their armor over their skill, but nevertheless, they are a force to be feared.\n\nMany among these men are descendents of the Rohirric companions of Wulf who settled in Byrig, and are loyal to Wulf's line. Nevertheless, other Dunlendings have also joined the Warband as a result of their deep loyalty to their king. Utterly steadfast, they wield large shields and spears that allow them to stand firm against the tides of the enemy. For while they may lack the ferocity and offensive skill of more traditional Dunlending warriors who fight in lighter armour, out of all in Dunland, they are the most skilled in holding the line. 
{Dunlending_Warband_descr_short}Elite heavily armoured spear-wielding wildmen infantry loyal to Wulf's line, clad in lamellar and holding a shield.

{Isenmach_Axemen}Hacheros de la guardia de Wulf
{Isenmach_Axemen_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Impetuous\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs, Forest, Snow\nTerrain malus: Barren\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nTo face the Wulfguard is said to be akin to facing the Judgment of Wulf himself. Wulf's closest friends and allies were gifted the finest armor and arms, from both his personal stores initially, and then the armories of Rohan. So fine, that Dunland lacked the skill and facilities to reproduce them and can only replace them by looting foreign armories. At Wulf's death, his closest friends and allies swore to protect his line so that one day, they would once again hold Edoras. Passing down their armor and arms as heirlooms, the Wulfingir is an elite force descended from these men, composed of axemen and pikes usually led by the King's right and left hand. It is said their arms and armour were restored and strengthened by the dwarves themselves in recognition of Dunland's aid to them during their long wanderings, which is why they have lasted for two centuries without decaying unlike most arms and armor forged by men.\n\nThese men wield and great two-handed Dunnish axes. In contrast to the Wulfguard Pikemen, these men are know for their ferocity and rage when it comes to battle, for wielding their axes, they often rush into enemy ranks dealing death with each swing of their weapons. Known by some as the Hammers of Dunland, they are more than capable of smashing the ranks of their foes and winning the day for their commander.  \n\nREQUIRES LANDMARK BUILDING.   
{Isenmach_Axemen_descr_short}Highly trained elite shock infantry, equipped with finely crafted armour and a two-handed axe.\n\nREQUIRES LANDMARK BUILDING.

{Isenmach_Raiders}Inquebrantables frekkalingir
{Isenmach_Raiders_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Good\nMovement Speed: 100%\nSecondary Attack: 7\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Forest, Snow\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nIf the Dunhird are those who keep to tradition, the Frekkalingir, the Children of Freca are those who reject them in favour of steeds. It is is said that before Wulf, Dunland had little cavalry except that which was mounted on ponies. However, Wulf's conquest of Rohan, and then Edoras allowed Dunland to capture many prize steeds, and from this grew Dunland's regular cavalry, a force that Wulf named in memeory of his father. For afterall, they are skilled in techniques passed that Freca developed, and passed down to Wulf, and then from Wulf to the original Frekkalingir, a unique combat style that blended Dunlending and Rohirric tactics, reflecting Freca's mixed heritage.\n\nThese men are largely descendents of Wulf's Rohirric companions, and and by far the most elite cavarly that Dunland can muster. The steeds they ride are the finest in Dunland, capable of bearing their armour, and are descended from either the prize war horses Wulf's companions brought to Rohan, or the ones that they claimed from Edoras itself. And their arms and armour are a fine match for the beasts they ride, for their armour is among the finest Dunland can produce, often even higher in quality than that of the King's Warband. And likewise, their spears are not wont to break easily and can pierce through armour and claim the lives of those who dare to stand against their wielders, and the swords they wield in melee are as finly crafted. In contrast to the Hill-Riders and Harriers, it is these fine steeds, weapons and armour that allow these men to execute charges, charges that should be feared for their ferocity by their foes. 
{Isenmach_Raiders_descr_short}Elite Frekkalingir cavalry able to outflank enemy's ranks and execute frontal charges.

{Dunland_Horsemen}Jinetes de las colinas frekkalingir
{Dunland_Horsemen_descr}Morale: Poor\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 90%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Forest, Snow\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nIf the Dunhird are those who keep to tradition, the Frekkalingir, the Children of Freca are those who reject them in favour of steeds. It is is said that before Wulf, Dunland had little cavalry except that which was mounted on ponies. However, Wulf's conquest of Rohan, and then Edoras allowed Dunland to capture many prize steeds, and from this grew Dunland's regular cavalry, a force that Wulf named in memeory of his father. For afterall, they are skilled in techniques passed that Freca developed, and passed down to Wulf, and then from Wulf to the original Frekkalingir, a unique combat style that blended Dunlending and Rohirric tactics, reflecting Freca's mixed heritage.\n\nThe majority of Frekkalingir are the Hill-Riders. Often native Dunlendings or new recruits, these men ride the hardy ponies that were virtually the only form of steed that the rough hills of Dunland can support naturally. Ligtly armoured, these men wield axes that are close kin to those that unmounted Dunlending warriors wield, and are poorly equipped to charge enemy lines or to engage in prolonged melee combat. Nevertheless, they are skilled at hit and run tactics, and can flank enemy lines to strike at units such as archers that are even more poorly equipped to engage in melee. And of course, they are skilled in hunting down routing troops. Nevertheless, while numerous, they have their limits, and a wise commander would not push them beyond them lest they flee. 
{Dunland_Horsemen_descr_short}Lightly armoured Frekkalingir cavalry, best used for hit and run tactics.

{Mounted_Warband}Aguiluchos frekkalingir
{Mounted_Warband_descr}Morale: Good\nMorale Response: Average\nMovement Speed: 100%\nTerrain bonus: Shrubs\nTerrain malus: Barren, Forest, Snow\n\nModel and unit by BillBaraka and Lerynian\n\nIf the Dunhird are those who keep to tradition, the Frekkalingir, the Children of Freca are those who reject them in favour of steeds. It is is said that before Wulf, Dunland had little cavalry except that which was mounted on ponies. However, Wulf's conquest of Rohan, and then Edoras allowed Dunland to capture many prize steeds, and from this grew Dunland's regular cavalry, a force that Wulf named in memeory of his father. For afterall, they are skilled in techniques passed that Freca developed, and passed down to Wulf, and then from Wulf to the original Frekkalingir, a unique combat style that blended Dunlending and Rohirric tactics, reflecting Freca's mixed heritage.\n\nIn contrast to the pony riding Hill-Riders, Frekkalingir Harriers are mounted on proper steeds. Those with wealth, or those who have proven their skill as Hill-Riders often serve as Harriers, and are equipped with skillfully crafted fur armour, and proper lances that are designed for mounted combat, in contrast to the axes of the Hill-Riders. Their fur vests themselves are a proof of their status as Harriers as Dunland turned to their traditional hiding and fur-crafting techniques to produce vests that allow them protection from the weather, wind and enemy blows, while the light design of the armour also allows them to move quickly on their steeds to pursue their foes. Furthermore, it is these men who best embody the tactics Freca taught Wulf, for the design of their spears are especially deadly to other steeds, allowing them to use a fusion of Dunlending and Rohirric methods to combat their Rohirric foes. However, although they are skilled in combating enemy cavarly, their weapons are a poor fit against infantry and they should not be deployed in melee against them except in dire need.
{Mounted_Warband_descr_short}Frekkalingir cavalry armed with heavy spears and medium armour.
